The daily field guide for digital media

AI Media Scout

2026-11-01 · All platforms · Open-source software

Today’s full expanded search supplies 53 detailed guides, including 27 completed from earlier screened entries, plus 18 additional eligible screened leads. It adds 44 distinct tools to the cumulative library. The plan spans 79 fields, 657 repository queries and 18 model tasks; new adjacencies include printmaking and museum/exhibition media. Live primary-source research supplements every field. New guides cover agent-authored media, neural timelines, audio, CAD, game creation and diagram/print graphics. Open integrations and skills are distinguished from proprietary hosts, paid inference and separately licensed weights/assets. Current setup failures and incomplete production routes remain explicit. No new entry earned Recommended or Ready to try, and no discovered app was installed or run. First guide means new to this library, not a launch today. All platforms were searched equally; actual documented support remains tool-specific. The full existing weekly library review was complete before collection, and each addition receives a current six-check source assessment. Raw unscreened candidates remain local and discovery is not an exhaustive census. Verified collection: 657/657 repository queries attempted; 656 successful, 1 partial, 0 failed and 122 bounded/truncated. All 18 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 17 watchlist fetches and 1 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Search and review counts

79 creative fields; 657/657 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 28503 distinct source candidates and 862 model leads. 53 detailed profiles and 18 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 1 partial repository queries, 122 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 2 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

Profiles are based on current primary documentation. Creative quality and performance claims are attributed to the developers unless hands-on testing is explicitly recorded. Unknown hardware requirements stay unknown. Code, model weights, hosted services, and required proprietary hosts can have different terms.

How it uses AI

A coding-agent workflow researches a topic, writes narration, synthesizes speech and authors Remotion scenes before FFmpeg assembles the video. [1] [3]

Introduction

A coding-agent workflow researches a topic, writes narration, synthesizes speech and authors Remotion scenes before FFmpeg assembles the video. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Narrated knowledge videos and animated explainers with an audio-led timeline, repeatable per-video assets and Chinese or English narration. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The README links publisher examples and describes multi-platform output. These examples were documented, not independently evaluated for factual accuracy, visual quality or platform acceptance. [1] [3]

Install

Install the full video-podcast-maker skill, or clone the repository. Prepare Python dependencies, FFmpeg and a Remotion project before requesting a topic-driven video. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install the full video-podcast-maker skill, or clone the repository. Prepare Python dependencies, FFmpeg and a Remotion project before requesting a topic-driven video.
npx skills add Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/skills/video-podcast-maker -g
pip install -r skills/video-podcast-maker/requirements.txt
npx create-video@latest my-video-project

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Choose a short topic and language; review the research and narration before synthesis.
  2. Select Edge or Azure speech, then inspect pronunciation and the generated timing.
  3. Preview the Remotion scenes, approve the final render, and inspect captions and audio in the MP4.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
No minimum RAM or VRAM is stated. The skill estimates about 2.2 GB of packages plus a 90 MB headless browser for a new Remotion project; media and output storage are additional.
Software
Python 3.8+, Node.js 18+, FFmpeg 4.0+, a compatible coding agent, Python requirements and a Remotion/Chromium rendering environment.
Platforms
The maintainer reports macOS testing and Linux compatibility. Windows operation is not established by this source.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: The integration is MIT. Agent/model access, Microsoft speech services and Remotion have separate terms; Edge synthesis is a remote service despite scripts running locally.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. The integration is MIT. Agent/model access, Microsoft speech services and Remotion have separate terms; Edge synthesis is a remote service despite scripts running locally.
  • Cost: Agent access/quota and any Azure speech or optional generation services are separate. The default Edge route is described as keyless; local rendering still consumes compute.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

Research and narration can contain errors. Preview and render approvals remain part of the published workflow. Broad publishing targets do not prove automated platform delivery or current acceptance. [1] [3]

How it uses AI

An agent coordinates language planning, generated collage keyframes, image-to-video, speech and music through Atlas Cloud, with local FFmpeg assembly. [1]

Introduction

An agent coordinates language planning, generated collage keyframes, image-to-video, speech and music through Atlas Cloud, with local FFmpeg assembly. [1]

What it is good for

Editorial paper-collage explainers or short ads, plus restyled talking-head footage, image-led stories and presenter inserts. [1]

Demo & examples

The publisher links a 30-second civilization film and several 60-second examples. The links establish examples exist, not independent review of quality, identity preservation or factual claims. [1]

Install

Clone the skill into a compatible coding agent, configure an Atlas Cloud key privately, and install FFmpeg/ffprobe plus Python and Pillow. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the skill into a compatible coding agent, configure an Atlas Cloud key privately, and install FFmpeg/ffprobe plus Python and Pillow.
git clone https://github.com/Alisa0808/vox-director.git
pip install pillow

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Request a short film with a topic, language, aspect ratio and duration.
  2. Approve the beat map and choose a style from the generated bake-off.
  3. Review generated posters before animation; inspect narration, captions and the assembled final MP4.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
A file/shell-capable coding agent, Atlas Cloud access, FFmpeg/ffprobe and Python 3 with Pillow. Exact Python, FFmpeg and OS versions are not specified.
Platforms
A macOS Homebrew example is documented. No validated Windows/Linux or browser-only execution matrix is published.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: The skill code is MIT; selected Google, Kling, Seedance, xAI and MiniMax endpoints are separate hosted services. Their availability, content/data terms and generated-media rights need workflow-specific review.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. The skill code is MIT; selected Google, Kling, Seedance, xAI and MiniMax endpoints are separate hosted services. Their availability, content/data terms and generated-media rights need workflow-specific review.
  • Cost: Paid hosted generation plus coding-agent access and local encoding. No universal per-film cost is established.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

The workflow is cloud dependent and model IDs can change. The claimed frame preservation or voice similarity is not independently verified. Local assembly does not make model inference private or offline. Open PR #10 proposes avoiding duplicate billable submissions after failures/timeouts. This is a current unresolved cost/retry concern in the reviewed source, not evidence that the proposed fix is shipped. [1] [10]

How it uses AI

An Android slicer exposes a DeepSeek-agent client and embedded geometry bridges; a separate harness can orchestrate photo-to-3D generation and return a mesh to the plate. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

An Android slicer exposes a DeepSeek-agent client and embedded geometry bridges; a separate harness can orchestrate photo-to-3D generation and return a mesh to the plate. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Phone-based inspection and preparation of AI-assisted models, with local slicing and separately configured remote generation. [1] [3] [4]

The README and assistant guide document the phone/harness/GPU architecture. The claimed generated STL is not evidence of a successful physical print or a validated snap fit. [1] [3] [4]

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware
The APK is about 500 MB; installed/model/output storage and phone RAM minima are not specified. The assistant guide describes an 8 GB GPU host and an observed roughly 5.7 GB VRAM pipeline peak, not a universal minimum.
Software
Android 10+ on arm64-v8a; embedded CuraEngine 5.14.0-alpha.0, PrusaSlicer 3.0.0-alpha11, OrcaSlicer 2.4.2 and Blender 3.6. AI needs a separately operated harness; photo-to-3D additionally uses a GPU host.
Platforms
Android ARM64 only: the source explicitly excludes other native platforms. The agent/harness/GPU hosts are separate machines, not phone-local model inference.

  • Commercial: Software use follows AGPL-3.0. The app is AGPL-3.0-or-later; some embedded components retain AGPL-3.0-only terms. The external model, harness, Blender and other tools retain their own licenses.
  • Cost: Phone/local compute and any separately selected agent/model hosting. Printer hardware and materials are separate.

Platforms

Software

Platforms
Source installer routes cover Windows and macOS/Linux; compatibility of individual OBS backends and AI hosts is separate.

  • Cost: Local OBS compute plus selected AI-host access; streaming-platform services remain separate.

How it uses AI

Eight agent skills coordinate code-built Three.js scenes and selected image/video generation, with browser checking and frame capture. [1]

Introduction

Eight agent skills coordinate code-built Three.js scenes and selected image/video generation, with browser checking and frame capture. [1]

What it is good for

Interactive 3D websites, scroll-controlled films, exploded views, cursor-following characters and transparent-video assets. [1]

Demo & examples

The README shows site examples and states the techniques/scripts were used on real projects. Those are publisher reports, not independent validation across browsers or generation providers. [1]

Install

Install selected skills with the documented CLI, start a new agent session, and prepare the Python/browser tools and FFmpeg needed for the chosen workflow. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install selected skills with the documented CLI, start a new agent session, and prepare the Python/browser tools and FFmpeg needed for the chosen workflow.
npx skills add Moh4696/open-3d-skills
pip install -r requirements.txt
python3 -m playwright install chromium webkit

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Begin with one Three.js scene or exploded-view request and explicit dimensions/interaction goals.
  2. For generated footage, choose a separate provider and inspect framing/alpha quality before integration.
  3. Run the project’s browser-review and capture workflow and verify the final target browser manually.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Node 22.20+, Python 3.9+, Playwright, Pillow, NumPy, OpenCV and FFmpeg. Some skills require separate image/video generation; code-scene/check/capture skills do not.
Platforms
Browser code has no build step, but toolchain and rendering still need a compatible local host. Safari-specific alpha-video claims need testing on the actual device.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Skill code/instructions are MIT. Three.js, supplied/imported assets, external model services and agent access are separately governed.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Skill code/instructions are MIT. Three.js, supplied/imported assets, external model services and agent access are separately governed.
  • Cost: Agent access and local browser/rendering compute; external generation costs depend on the selected route.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

These are workflow instructions and helper scripts, not a bundled local video model. Exact RAM, VRAM, disk and OS minima are not documented, and code-built assets do not automatically resolve external asset rights. [1]

Introduction

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  3. Reload exports through Blender and Three.js, then verify Roblox behavior in Roblox Studio separately.

Platforms
The workflow is host-portable, but actual tool availability and supported native platforms must be checked. No universal auto-discovery or proprietary connector is required by the instructions.

Hardware & software

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Rust 1.95+ for source builds. Windows needs MSVC C++ tools; macOS needs the Xcode command-line linker; Linux uses documented X11/Wayland and Vulkan/GL libraries. Models/agents are external.
Platforms
Packaged routes: Windows x86_64, Apple Silicon macOS and Linux x86_64. macOS builds are ad-hoc signed, not notarized; no Intel Mac package is established in this release matrix.

How it uses AI

Introduction

An agent authors a film as editable code, while local rendering engines replay that code frame by frame and produce technical QA artifacts. [1]

What it is good for

Code-authored motion graphics, technical explainers, 3D films and synthesized sound with reusable blocks and optional brand kits. [1]

Demo & examples

The README describes annotated films, contact sheets and automated checks. A score assigned by the producing agent is not independent creative-quality approval or proof of determinism on another host. [1]

Install

Install the agent skills, then clone the full rendering repository. Install npm dependencies and the required browser, run the documented doctor, and choose an engine whose dependencies and terms fit the project. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install the agent skills, then clone the full rendering repository. Install npm dependencies and the required browser, run the documented doctor, and choose an engine whose dependencies and terms fit the project.
npx skills add FrameCoreWorks/FCWM
npm install
npx playwright install chromium
node scripts/fcwm.mjs doctor

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  3. Review the MP4/preview and technical QA; inspect each requested aspect ratio and any transparent output.

Node.js 22+ and FFmpeg; Chromium for browser rendering. uv is needed for motion-reel Python scores; Manim and Blender routes have additional engine-specific dependencies.
Platforms
The command is documented across macOS, Windows and Linux; GitHub Actions is an optional user-operated render route. This research did not enable or run it.

How it uses AI

MediaPipe hand tracking and a trained random-forest gesture classifier map camera-observed gestures to polyphonic notes and expressive synth controls. [1]

Introduction

MediaPipe hand tracking and a trained random-forest gesture classifier map camera-observed gestures to polyphonic notes and expressive synth controls. [1]

What it is good for

Camera-based musical sketches, gesture-performance prototypes and guided instrument experiments without a physical keyboard. [1]

Demo & examples

The source documents guided/free/piano/theremin modes and a synthetic simulator. The simulator bypasses real tracking, and publisher accuracy/latency claims are not independent live-performance measurements. [1]

Install

Clone the repository, prepare Python 3.9+ and its requirements, then start a small free-instrument or simulator session with working audio output. [1]

  2. Clone the repository, prepare Python 3.9+ and its requirements, then start a small free-instrument or simulator session with working audio output.
git clone https://github.com/idriss-hamza/hand-gesture-instrument.git
pip install -r requirements.txt
python3 run_instrument.py --free
python3 run_instrument.py --simulate

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. First verify audio and the simulator, then switch to a real webcam.
  2. Try a few documented gestures and inspect tracking under the actual lighting and framing.
  3. Adjust hand roles/instrument modes and listen for missed notes, latency or accidental changes before a performance.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
A webcam is optional for simulation but required for actual gesture performance; audio output is required. CPU, RAM, VRAM and storage minima are not documented.
Software
Python 3.9+, OpenCV, MediaPipe HandLandmarker and the project dependencies; webcam and audio output. Camera-free simulation is available. Exact model-file provenance and dependency terms need further review.
Platforms
No validated operating-system, camera or audio-driver matrix is published. A Python interface alone does not prove support on every desktop or edge device.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Project code is MIT. MediaPipe assets, trained classifier data/model and any audio/sample dependencies need their own terms; the root grant does not establish all training-data rights.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Project code is MIT. MediaPipe assets, trained classifier data/model and any audio/sample dependencies need their own terms; the root grant does not establish all training-data rights.
  • Cost: Local camera/audio compute; no paid model service is documented for the core route.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

The stated perfect canonical-gesture test score and sub-millimeter tracking are publisher claims, not evidence of accuracy on arbitrary hands/cameras. Synthetic data and geometric heuristics can leave real-world gaps. [1]

How it uses AI

An AI agent writes musical briefs into YAML, MIDI and code-based parts; deterministic synthesis, mixing and analysis let it inspect and revise the resulting audio. [1]

Introduction

An AI agent writes musical briefs into YAML, MIDI and code-based parts; deterministic synthesis, mixing and analysis let it inspect and revise the resulting audio. [1]

What it is good for

Headless music/sound sketches with versioned text sources, repeatable renders and machine-readable loudness/spectral feedback. [1]

Demo & examples

The repository includes example-sketch and documented output locations for stems, master WAV/FLAC and analysis JSON. These are reproducibility instructions, not an independently judged listening evaluation. [1]

Install

Prepare uv and GNU Make, clone the project and build the example track. uv obtains Python 3.12+ and dependencies. Add optional external renderers only for the sources used by a track. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare uv and GNU Make, clone the project and build the example track. uv obtains Python 3.12+ and dependencies. Add optional external renderers only for the sources used by a track.
git clone https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma.git
make all TRACK=example-sketch
uv run stemma new night-drive --title "Night Drive"
uv run stemma all night-drive

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Render the example and inspect its analysis and stems.
  2. Ask the agent to write a short musical brief into a new track spec and part sources.
  3. Listen to the mix as well as reading metrics; revise arrangement, dynamics and timbre before mastering.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
uv with Python 3.12+ and GNU Make. The example has a built-in synthesizer; its optional FluidSynth pad is skipped when unavailable. sfizz, Csound, Faust/C++ and LilyPond are optional route-specific tools.
Platforms
Debian/Ubuntu package examples are documented. Exact tested Windows/macOS/native audio support is not established by this guide.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Own code is MIT. Optional effects/renderers, soundfonts and samples retain their own licenses; an open MIDI/YAML format does not grant rights to a chosen sound library.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Own code is MIT. Optional effects/renderers, soundfonts and samples retain their own licenses; an open MIDI/YAML format does not grant rights to a chosen sound library.
  • Cost: Local synthesis/rendering plus agent access and any separately licensed instruments or samples.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

How it uses AI

Introduction

How it uses AI

An MCP server gives AI clients a common edit-tool surface backed by FFmpeg, optional proprietary editors or timeline interchange formats. [1] [3]

Introduction

An MCP server gives AI clients a common edit-tool surface backed by FFmpeg, optional proprietary editors or timeline interchange formats. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Agent-directed rough cuts and timeline construction with a backend chosen for the actual editing environment. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The README documents a quick-start ping and 65 editing tools. Connectivity or a tool count does not establish a visually correct finished film or identical behavior across backends. [1] [3]

Install

Clone the current repository, create a Python environment in server/, install the source package and configure the MCP client for a selected backend. The package is not yet published on PyPI. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the current repository, create a Python environment in server/, install the source package and configure the MCP client for a selected backend. The package is not yet published on PyPI.
git clone https://github.com/RFingAdam/rushcut.git
pip install -e .

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Prepare a short owned clip and select the FFmpeg backend for the initial connection check.
  2. Ask the agent to inspect media and make one simple timeline operation.
  3. Render or export to the intended editor, then check timing, effects and audio in the real output.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3.11+, FFmpeg and ffprobe; an MCP stdio client. Local transcription is an optional whisper extra. VEGAS/Resolve or interchange consumers require their own installations.
Platforms
The source documents Windows and Unix-style setup alternatives. Backend/editor availability varies by operating system and is not established by MCP support alone.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: The complete file contains an MIT grant followed by affiliation and voluntary CC0-reference notes; it does not add a non-commercial condition. Selected editor, model and codec terms remain separate.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. The complete file contains an MIT grant followed by affiliation and voluntary CC0-reference notes; it does not add a non-commercial condition. Selected editor, model and codec terms remain separate.
  • Cost: Local processing plus selected AI service and any required editor license.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

Backend parity, editor scripting configuration and export fidelity require review. No published package means source setup is necessary; a generic MCP command does not guarantee editor-specific behavior. The current notice distinguishes DaVinci Resolve Studio live scripting from free Resolve 21.1, which lacks that scripting API; the open integration does not grant the proprietary host. [1] [3] [3]

How it uses AI

Introduction

A host agent organizes text into characters, scenes, dialogue and deterministic animation tracks, while a separate local CLI/editor previews and exports the story. [1]

What it is good for

Editable visual-novel-style dialogue videos with reusable assets and continuing local revisions. [1]

Install the vnve skill and the separately released matching CLI. Verify the binary SHA-256, put it on PATH and run doctor; skill installation alone does not install the runtime. [1]

vnve doctor

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  3. Export in a browser with actual codec support and preserve the editable project and asset-source records.

Software

Platforms
The documented binary matrix names Apple Silicon macOS, Linux x64 and Windows x64; actual release assets govern availability. Desktop Chrome/Edge are recommended by the source for export.

How it uses AI

A SOLIDWORKS add-in embeds the Claude Code workflow so a model can plan and execute native CAD operations with user-reviewed plans. [1]

Introduction

A SOLIDWORKS add-in embeds the Claude Code workflow so a model can plan and execute native CAD operations with user-reviewed plans. [1]

What it is good for

Parametric part/design prototypes inside an existing SOLIDWORKS workstation, retaining the native modeling environment. [1]

Demo & examples

The README documents a task-pane workflow and plan/build checks. A prerelease label and promotional modeling examples do not establish independent geometric or manufacturing accuracy. [1]

Install

Install the companion solidworks-api skill, build the source against the local SOLIDWORKS interop files, register the add-in and sign into the official Claude client from the panel. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install the companion solidworks-api skill, build the source against the local SOLIDWORKS interop files, register the add-in and sign into the official Claude client from the panel.
git clone https://github.com/Nilove2018/cad-master-solidworks.git
./build.ps1
./install.ps1

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Save a copy of a small model and describe dimensions/process in Plan mode.
  2. Review the plan before allowing the agent to construct or revise geometry.
  3. Inspect actual features, dimensions and rebuilds in SOLIDWORKS rather than relying on the agent’s completion text.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Windows 10/11 64-bit, SOLIDWORKS 2025, Python 3.9+ with pywin32 and WebView2 Runtime. A supported Claude account/Console access and Claude Code are required.
Platforms
Windows is documented; SOLIDWORKS versions other than 2025 are explicitly untested. No macOS/Linux native route is established.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Own code is GPL-3.0 with a section-7 permission to combine/distribute with SOLIDWORKS API interop and WebView2 components. Name/icons have separate trademark scope; SOLIDWORKS and Claude remain separately licensed services/software.
  • Commercial: Software use follows GPL-3.0. Own code is GPL-3.0 with a section-7 permission to combine/distribute with SOLIDWORKS API interop and WebView2 components. Name/icons have separate trademark scope; SOLIDWORKS and Claude remain separately licensed services/software.
  • Cost: SOLIDWORKS entitlement, selected Claude access and local workstation compute.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

The source build downloads compiler/SDK dependencies and registration needs elevated Windows rights. Automation can affect the open CAD session; independent setup and creative-use evidence remain missing. [1]

A ComfyUI timeline editor directs LTX 2.5 video generation with scene durations, prompts and optional first/last-frame conditioning. [1]

Introduction

A ComfyUI timeline editor directs LTX 2.5 video generation with scene durations, prompts and optional first/last-frame conditioning. [1]

What it is good for

Planning several generated shots in one visual editor and choosing generated, uploaded or silent audio per clip. [1]

Demo & examples

The README links image-to-video, multi-scene and first/last-frame examples with node screenshots. They are publisher demonstrations, not independent quality or long-sequence consistency evidence. [1]

Install

Install the node through ComfyUI Manager/CLI or clone it under custom_nodes. Restart ComfyUI, add HappyLatentVideo, and prepare the separate LTX 2.5 models and starter workflow. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install the node through ComfyUI Manager/CLI or clone it under custom_nodes. Restart ComfyUI, add HappyLatentVideo, and prepare the separate LTX 2.5 models and starter workflow.
comfy node install happylatent

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Load the starter workflow and verify every model/encoder/VAE path.
  2. Create one short scene with a prompt and owned reference frame, then inspect its result.
  3. Add another scene and check boundaries, duration and selected audio before a larger render.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
A compatible ComfyUI environment, LTX 2.5 model, text encoder, video VAE and audio VAE. Exact ComfyUI/Python versions and hardware minima are not stated in the reviewed README.
Platforms
The browser editor depends on a working local/remote ComfyUI backend. No validated native OS/GPU matrix is published for this node.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Node code is GPL-3.0. LTX weights, encoders, VAEs and any source audio/images retain separate terms; their availability and commercial scope need review.
  • Commercial: Software use follows GPL-3.0. Node code is GPL-3.0. LTX weights, encoders, VAEs and any source audio/images retain separate terms; their availability and commercial scope need review.
  • Cost: ComfyUI compute/storage and selected model access; no universal model-download size or render price is documented.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

An example timeline does not establish consistent characters, clean audio joins or enough memory for every scene length. This review did not run the node or install model assets. [1]

Introduction

A visual ComfyUI layout node compiles object/text/character boxes into the official MOSS-Image conditioning format for neural image generation. [1]

  3. Generate a small example and compare composition/text with the intended layout before iterating.

How it uses AI

An MCP-accessible audio toolchain lets an AI agent audit, repair and master files through measured deterministic DSP operations. [1]

Introduction

An MCP-accessible audio toolchain lets an AI agent audit, repair and master files through measured deterministic DSP operations. [1]

What it is good for

Preparing delivery masters with explicit technical pass/fail reports, and optionally exporting stems, MIDI or a REAPER session. [1]

Demo & examples

The README documents a delivery report and sample commands. A target-spec pass measures selected technical conditions; it is not a universal streaming-platform acceptance or musical-quality certificate. [1]

Install

Build the Rust CLI or MCP server from source. Configure the server’s allowed music root and connect an MCP client; optional plugin/REAPER paths need their own toolchains. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Build the Rust CLI or MCP server from source. Configure the server’s allowed music root and connect an MCP client; optional plugin/REAPER paths need their own toolchains.
cargo build --release -p forge-mcp
cargo run --release --bin forge -- deliver mix.wav -o out/master.wav --target spotify

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Use an owned mix and inspect the input audit before applying repairs.
  2. Compare the repaired/mastered output against the original and review loudness/true-peak findings.
  3. Listen end to end and validate the actual destination’s needs before delivery.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Rust toolchain/Cargo and the chosen build path; the README identifies Rust 2024 edition but does not establish a universal compiler/hardware minimum. Optional REAPER integration targets 7.82+.
Platforms
CLI, MCP, VST3/CLAP and native REAPER JSFX are different routes. A complete tested OS/plugin-host matrix is not established in this review.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Own code is Apache-2.0. Optional analysis/separation/transcription dependencies, plugin SDKs, DAWs and source media retain separate terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Own code is Apache-2.0. Optional analysis/separation/transcription dependencies, plugin SDKs, DAWs and source media retain separate terms.
  • Cost: Local audio processing and selected AI-host access; optional DAW/plugin requirements can add costs.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

The core DSP is not itself a generative model; model reasoning enters through the documented agent/MCP route. Technical reports do not replace listening, and optional session outputs need separate review. A current closed report and follow-up PR narrow GPU/Colab functionality to real two-stem separation; the advertised YuE2 generation branch returned static JSON and was removed rather than implemented. Default stem separation may fall back to DSP; the new require-neural route is explicit. The follow-up says its notebook was not run on Colab. [1] [6] [13]

How it uses AI

A local analyzer measures cuts/audio/captions while an AI agent interprets the frames and maps a reference format onto the creator’s own media. [1]

Introduction

A local analyzer measures cuts/audio/captions while an AI agent interprets the frames and maps a reference format onto the creator’s own media. [1]

What it is good for

Studying a short video’s structure and building an editable plan or a new cut with owned clips and newly cleared sound. [1]

Demo & examples

The README specifies EDIT-SPEC.md, TEMPLATE.md, captions and an OTIO timeline. A finished MP4 additionally depends on FilmCraft; analysis files alone are not a completed edit. [1]

Install

Prepare Node 18+ and Git, install the project and use its setup wizard for FFmpeg and speech/text models. A matching prebuilt binary is used where available; source fallback needs Rust 1.94+. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare Node 18+ and Git, install the project and use its setup wizard for FFmpeg and speech/text models. A matching prebuilt binary is used where available; source fallback needs Rust 1.94+.
git clone https://github.com/bankThanabat/ViralCraft.git
npm install
npm run setup
npx viralcraft doctor

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Place an authorized reference in inbox/ and your own clips/sounds in assets/.
  2. Have the agent inspect measured timing and frames, then review its clip/caption/music plan.
  3. Use the FilmCraft handoff or another editor to rebuild; inspect the final export and retain the provenance of assets.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Node.js 18+, Git and FFmpeg; setup installs approximately 90 MB of default speech/text models. Multilingual alternatives are documented as 0.5/1.5/3.1 GB. Source builds need Rust 1.94+.
Platforms
Release builds target macOS, Linux and Windows. Prebuilt x64 binaries require AVX2; older CPUs need a source build. Native Mac speech and remote voice services have different availability.

  • Cost: Local analysis and agent access; optional paid voices or downstream editing services have separate terms.

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3.10+, FFmpeg/ffprobe; Node 20+ and Chromium/Playwright for starter rendering. The agent and any chosen image/voice models are external.
Platforms
The source includes macOS and Ubuntu/Debian setup examples. Windows or other native support is not independently established by this guide.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Original code/instructions are MIT. Case-study product names, screens, fonts and third-party images/data are explicitly outside that grant.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Original code/instructions are MIT. Case-study product names, screens, fonts and third-party images/data are explicitly outside that grant.
  • Cost: Local frame rendering/encoding and selected agent/model services; no universal render speed or production cost is documented.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

A deterministic seek/render interface does not guarantee a good story, accessible typography or artifact-free export. The agent’s acceptance gates need real output evidence, not completion text. [1]

How it uses AI

Research notebooks encode an audio corpus with a neural codec and distill a small query encoder for retrieval-based granular resynthesis. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

Research notebooks encode an audio corpus with a neural codec and distill a small query encoder for retrieval-based granular resynthesis. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Researching corpus instruments and lower-cost neural-latent retrieval, rather than installing a finished general-purpose music app. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

The source links a paper, 4:37 walkthrough, desktop instrument history and saved measurement results. The included notebooks use generated demo signals; original corpus audio and rendered clips are deliberately absent. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Prepare uv, synchronize the pinned Python 3.12 environment and open the notebooks. Keep the offline notebook route distinct from the separately documented JUCE/plugin/Elk-board build. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare uv, synchronize the pinned Python 3.12 environment and open the notebooks. Keep the offline notebook route distinct from the separately documented JUCE/plugin/Elk-board build.
uv sync
uv run jupyter lab notebooks/

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Read the corpus-packing notebook and inspect the generated demo signal and stored results.
  2. Follow the student-distillation/parity notebooks with legally usable audio if extending the experiment.
  3. Evaluate retrieval and listening results separately; do not treat saved board measurements as a new device run.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
uv and Python 3.12 for the notebook route. Teacher weights download on first use. Optional plugin/board work adds JUCE, RTNeural, Elk/SUSHI and build-system dependencies.
Platforms
The desktop experiment began on an M1 Max laptop; the selected notebook instructions do not establish a complete OS matrix. The Elk Stomp route is separate hardware.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4]

  • Weights: Author source is MIT. music2latent code and weights and the supplied distilled weights are non-commercial; some training material is research-only. Elk’s extension header lacks an upstream license file, and plugin redistribution has separate JUCE/SDK obligations.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Author source is MIT. music2latent code and weights and the supplied distilled weights are non-commercial; some training material is research-only. Elk’s extension header lacks an upstream license file, and plugin redistribution has separate JUCE/SDK obligations.
  • Cost: Local research compute/storage and optional board/SDK entitlements; the documented non-commercial dependencies restrict monetized use.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3] [4]

Limitations

This is a source research reproduction route, not commercially cleared end-to-end software or a turnkey instrument. Source audio, packs, latents and parts of the board-control glue are omitted; permission gaps and listening quality remain explicit. [1] [3] [4]

How it uses AI

Neural speech enhancement uses phonetic representations and vocoders, with StuPASE adding flow-matching acoustic enhancement for noisy/reverberant recordings. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6] [7] [8]

Introduction

Neural speech enhancement uses phonetic representations and vocoders, with StuPASE adding flow-matching acoustic enhancement for noisy/reverberant recordings. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6] [7] [8]

What it is good for

Comparing speech-cleanup approaches for human-reviewed narration or interview restoration, scoped to the released checkpoints. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6] [7] [8]

Demo & examples

Primary demo pages and retrained-checkpoint tables are available. Released checkpoints use revised training data, so original-paper metrics cannot be silently transferred. No local listening or inference run was performed. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6] [7] [8]

Install

Clone the family source, review the subproject inference guide and obtain its three checkpoints. Resolve the contradictory requirements before building an environment; the listed file is not a working one-command install as written. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6] [7] [8]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the family source, review the subproject inference guide and obtain its three checkpoints. Resolve the contradictory requirements before building an environment; the listed file is not a working one-command install as written.
python -m inference.inference -I input_audio -O enhanced_audio -D cpu

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6] [7] [8]

  1. Prepare a short authorized speech recording and the correct subproject/checkpoints.
  2. Run the documented inference entry point only after resolving compatible dependencies.
  3. Compare enhanced and original speech for changed words, identity and artifacts; preserve the source recording.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6] [7] [8]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
PyTorch/torchaudio and the listed audio libraries. The current requirements file pins both torch 2.4.1 and 2.1.1, and both torchaudio 2.4.1 and 2.1.1, which conflict. StuPASE’s model card scopes input to 16 kHz mono speech.
Platforms
The inference flag documents CUDA devices and CPU. Native OS support, MPS behavior and real-time operation are not established; training on two RTX 4090s is not an inference minimum.

Introduction

Introduction

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Source minimum: NVIDIA host with 64 GB RAM and 150 GB free disk. Tested/recommended: DGX Spark with 128 GB unified memory; 1 TB+ disk for multiple models, with the general model described as roughly 37 GB.
Software
Ubuntu 22.04+, Docker 27+ with Compose v2, NVIDIA Container Toolkit, Python 3 and curl; initial Internet downloads. Some models may require Hugging Face access.
Platforms
Tested/recommended by the source on DGX Spark GB10/aarch64 with DGX OS 7. Other recent NVIDIA x86_64 hosts are expected but explicitly untested. No macOS/Windows native inference route is documented.

  • Cost: Substantial local GPU hardware/storage/electricity and any selected gated model or service terms; no hosted per-job price is established.

How it uses AI

An agent combines text-free AI background art with real-font HTML typography and scripted thumbnail/layout checks to make repository cover images. [1]

Introduction

An agent combines text-free AI background art with real-font HTML typography and scripted thumbnail/layout checks to make repository cover images. [1]

What it is good for

Batch Open Graph/social cards with CJK typography and readable 1280×640 artwork. [1]

Demo & examples

The source includes a generated cover, passing thumbnail sheet and an intentionally rejected cluttered example. OCR and layout passes do not independently validate the visual concept or factual headline. [1]

Install

Clone the skill into a compatible agent directory and prepare Python 3, Pillow, Chrome/Chromium and a suitable CJK font. The raster artwork comes from the selected external image-generation workflow. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the skill into a compatible agent directory and prepare Python 3, Pillow, Chrome/Chromium and a suitable CJK font. The raster artwork comes from the selected external image-generation workflow.
git clone https://github.com/liush2yuxjtu/github-social-preview-ai.git
python3 scripts/render.py examples/covers.json --out-dir out
python3 scripts/verify.py out/my-repo.png

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Have the agent propose text-free art and an accurate headline.
  2. Render a small covers.json batch and inspect the full card and 320×160 thumbnail.
  3. Resolve verification failures and manually upload only an approved image.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3 + Pillow, Google Chrome or Chromium and a CJK font such as Noto Sans CJK. The README does not specify exact minor versions or an integrated model backend.
Platforms
Agent folder conventions cover Claude Code, Cursor and Codex; a tested operating-system matrix is not documented.

  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. The skill and renderer are MIT. Image-generation service/model terms, input art and font licenses remain separate.
  • Cost: Local rendering plus the chosen agent/image-model costs.

How it uses AI

A Windows media library includes optional local CLIP tagging, MDX-Net music/voice separation and Real-ESRGAN upscaling alongside multiscreen playback and beat montages. [1] [3]

Introduction

A Windows media library includes optional local CLIP tagging, MDX-Net music/voice separation and Real-ESRGAN upscaling alongside multiscreen playback and beat montages. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Organizing owned footage and preparing reviewable video-wall or music-montage sessions, with explicit separation between conventional playback and neural processing. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The README describes nine-screen playback, mosaics and a 30-second upscale test. These are publisher feature descriptions; no independent live-show, synchronization or neural-quality test was established. [1] [3]

Install

Use the official Windows release installer, or the documented source launcher with Python 3.11+, 64-bit VLC and FFmpeg on PATH. Download only the optional models needed for the intended feature. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use the official Windows release installer, or the documented source launcher with Python 3.11+, 64-bit VLC and FFmpeg on PATH. Download only the optional models needed for the intended feature.
git clone https://github.com/pierreoliviermarcel-ui/phoque-al.git
lancer_phoqueal.bat

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Add a small folder of authorized media and verify indexing/playback.
  2. Try optional tagging or one short upscale, checking suggestions and original preservation.
  3. Save a small display/montage session and test actual screens, audio and timing before a venue run.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Windows x64 and a Vulkan-capable graphics card recommended for neural upscaling. Minimum RAM, VRAM and total disk footprint are not documented.
Software
Windows installer bundles VLC and FFmpeg. Source route needs Python 3.11+, VLC 64-bit and FFmpeg; optional AI files are downloaded separately.
Platforms
Windows 10/11 x64. A Vulkan-capable NVIDIA, AMD or Intel GPU is recommended for upscaling; no macOS/Linux application route is documented.

  • Commercial: Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPLv3-or-later own code. The complete third-party notice separates LGPL/GPL bundled components, CLIP/Real-ESRGAN/Upscayl terms and optional archive tools. It explicitly does not establish a separate license for the MDX-Net Kim Vocal 2 model; music-removal rights remain unresolved.

How it uses AI

An IINA plugin transcribes local video audio through local Whisper-family engines, caches native subtitle tracks and exports SRT for review. [1]

Introduction

An IINA plugin transcribes local video audio through local Whisper-family engines, caches native subtitle tracks and exports SRT for review. [1]

What it is good for

Private rough transcription and subtitle preparation for authorized local video on macOS. [1]

Demo & examples

The README reports an M1 Pro large-v3-q5_0 timing and describes incremental cues and cache behavior. Those owner measurements are not independent accuracy, language coverage or accessibility validation. [1]

Install

Open the official .iinaplgz release in IINA, or package from source. Prepare Xcode command-line tools and a chosen local speech engine/model. The README conflicts on whether whisper-cli is fetched automatically or must be installed with Homebrew; manual engine installation is a documented route. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Open the official .iinaplgz release in IINA, or package from source. Prepare Xcode command-line tools and a chosen local speech engine/model. The README conflicts on whether whisper-cli is fetched automatically or must be installed with Homebrew; manual engine installation is a documented route.
git clone https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub.git
bash scripts/package.sh
open dist/io.github.xinmin.iina-sub.iinaplgz
brew install whisper.cpp

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Open a small local video and choose the correct speech language/model.
  2. Review the early cues, speaker names and chunk-boundary repetitions.
  3. Export SRT beside the video and correct text/timing before any accessibility or publishing use.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Model files listed at 141 MB (base), 465 MB (small), 514 MB (medium quantized), 547 MB (large-v3-turbo quantized) or 1031 MB (large-v3 quantized). These are model sizes, not total disk/RAM requirements.
Software
macOS 13+, IINA 1.3+, Xcode command-line tools, whisper-cli or another configured supported local engine and model. Exact CPU/RAM minima are not stated.
Platforms
macOS/IINA only in this documented route. An M1 Pro timing is supplied; it does not establish a tested Intel/Apple-Silicon matrix for every engine.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Plugin source is MIT. IINA, Whisper engines, downloaded model distributions and any custom engine retain separate grants. The full current grant for every selectable model was not independently reviewed here.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Plugin source is MIT. IINA, Whisper engines, downloaded model distributions and any custom engine retain separate grants. The full current grant for every selectable model was not independently reviewed here.
  • Cost: Local compute/model storage; no cloud transcription key is required for the documented local engine.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

The source contradicts itself about automatic engine downloads and first-run behavior. Models can omit fillers or mishear language/names; chunk boundaries may repeat words. A subtitle export does not certify accessibility, and local processing claims do not replace a security audit. [1]

How it uses AI

An agent editing suite combines local neural transcription/denoising with deterministic cuts, captions, graphics and a separate model-assisted review pack. [1] [3]

Introduction

An agent editing suite combines local neural transcription/denoising with deterministic cuts, captions, graphics and a separate model-assisted review pack. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Turning short talking-head footage into inspectable edits with editable files, technical QA and explicit human review. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The README links a raw/edited demo whose presenter is AI-generated and mistakes are planted. The edit is an author example; neither that demo nor an Editor-in-Chief model score establishes independent creative quality. [1] [3]

Install

Install the source package with uv, prepare FFmpeg, then review the optional renderer/denoiser setup prompts. Motion graphics need Node 22+; Linux picture-text QA also needs Tesseract. Connect the skills to the chosen agent. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install the source package with uv, prepare FFmpeg, then review the optional renderer/denoiser setup prompts. Motion graphics need Node 22+; Linux picture-text QA also needs Tesseract. Connect the skills to the chosen agent.
uv tool install git+https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue
altocue setup

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Give the agent a short authorized source clip, brief and output format.
  2. Inspect transcript, proposed cuts, reframing and captions before final rendering.
  3. Review the full video and technical gates; treat model review as a separate fallible opinion and approve manually.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Source suggests 16 GB memory as comfortable and about 3.5 GB disk, including roughly 2.5 GB speech model; transcription uses about 4 GB memory. These are documented estimates, not verified minimums.
Software
uv and FFmpeg; Node.js 22+ for motion graphics; Tesseract for Linux picture-text QA. Apple Silicon uses parakeet-mlx; elsewhere the source uses faster-whisper. Optional DeepFilterNet, HyperFrames and Chrome add dependencies.
Platforms
Apple Silicon and Linux dependency paths are documented; a complete independently reproduced OS matrix is not established. Hosted provider paths vary in live-test depth.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: Own source is MIT. The complete license inventory separates fonts, model grants, FFmpeg builds and optional HyperFrames. HyperFrames bundles non-open-source GSAP even though Altocue says its graphics do not call it; Chrome and external AI hosts/services have separate terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Own source is MIT. The complete license inventory separates fonts, model grants, FFmpeg builds and optional HyperFrames. HyperFrames bundles non-open-source GSAP even though Altocue says its graphics do not call it; Chrome and external AI hosts/services have separate terms.
  • Cost: Local compute plus the chosen coding agent. Hosted generation/review is optional and billed separately on the user’s own key.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

Static per-shot crops cannot follow a walking subject. Heavy echo/clipping cannot be repaired reliably. zumen
MIT		Inspectable diagrams, lighting plots and placement drawings whose human corrections survive regeneration.
An MCP-connected AI agent proposes YAML diagram changes while a human approves diffs and retains layout pins in a minimal desktop editor.

Excluded and unresolved findings

These findings are retained as research notes and are not part of the eligible tool library.

  • RoboExhibit license conflict · needs-license-review: The current README calls the project GPLv3, while the complete root software license is Apache-2.0. Its concrete LLM/Unity museum-guide route is promising, but the conflicting software-license identity and separately hosted Unity frontend need clarification before inclusion. Source
  • SMV Viewer Prototype · excluded: The complete root license explicitly covers the page, JavaScript and compressed scenes with CC BY-NC-ND 4.0. Its non-commercial/no-derivatives conditions do not qualify as open-source software. Separate decoder/vendor grants do not relicense the whole player. Source
  • Dramai license identity · needs-license-review: The complete root license deletes wording from Apache Section 6 and changes Section 9 liability wording to support. The recognized Apache label does not settle this modified grant; clarification is required before a new eligible guide. Source
  • eian-riso-broll · excluded: The full software grant restricts use to personal/non-commercial contexts and prohibits redistribution; organization/commercial use needs a separate grant. Popularity and printmaking relevance do not remove those terms. Source
  • Infinode · excluded: The current complete software license is PolyForm Noncommercial; its creative workflow does not meet this project’s open-source software policy. Source
  • AgenticCAD current software · excluded: The current software grant is PolyForm Noncommercial, rather than a qualifying open-source grant. Source
  • VideoNeuMat code · excluded: The original software is licensed CC BY-NC 4.0. A separately Apache-licensed dependency does not relicense this non-commercial project code. Source
  • LyriFont · needs-license-review: The primary page establishes a creative AI typography workflow, but a complete qualifying software license was not established in the inspected repository. Source
  • Museum AI candidates · needs-license-review: Museum semantic-search and exhibition candidates were researched, but complete qualifying grants were not established and the proposed semantic-search demo URL returned 404. They were not counted as eligible leads. Source
  • EmbroideryAI-Research current release · excluded: The README explicitly says there is no image-to-embroidery ML model or working GUI agent. Its current deterministic codec research does not establish an implemented creative AI workflow. Source
  • PoseForge current implementation · excluded: The reviewed current route provides deterministic proxy geometry; the described model/agent integration is planned. AI-art subject matter alone is insufficient. Source
  • AI Foley SFX Generator implementation · needs-license-review: The download-oriented README makes broad claims but this pass did not establish a verifiable open implementation and model/source chain. Its metadata grant alone does not qualify the advertised executable. Source
  • OpenSuno-YuE2 current stack · excluded: The complete third-party notice assigns substantial implementation files as well as weights to non-commercial terms. Its MIT integration does not make the whole current stack open source. Existing historical entries and warnings are preserved. Source
  • Varen current binary · excluded: The current repository describes a private product/release portal with removed source. The existing library’s current-license warning is preserved; the historical AGPL entry does not qualify this new binary. Source
  • Palmier Pro current binary · excluded: The source distinguishes historical GPL code through 0.7.6 from later proprietary binaries. Current proprietary releases are not newly presented as open source. Source
